## Coverage ledger

| Week | Topic | Status | Actual evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Diagnostic prerequisites: algebra, derivative, rank, conditional probability, quantified proof | taught | Self-contained diagnostic definitions and five solved/rubric-scored tasks; Week 1 A/B forms replace one another. |
| 2 | Proof language, functions, induction | taught | Five proof-focused exercises with full witness/counterexample and pointwise rubric. |
| 3 | Euclidean geometry, inequalities, identifiability | taught | Cauchy–Schwarz proof and five transfer/proof tasks; finite-dimensional setup is explicit. |
| 4 | Single-variable calculus, finite differences, Taylor remainder | taught | Product rule proved from difference quotients; failure of differentiability and bounded Taylor remainder are solved. |
| 5 | Vector spaces, maps, rank–nullity | taught | General rank–nullity proof, constructed maps, and an identifiability theorem task. |
| 6 | Orthogonal projection and least squares | taught | Projection theorem with existence/uniqueness proof; normal equations and non-unique coefficients treated explicitly. |
| 7 | Spectral theorem, PSD, covariance geometry | taught | Inductive spectral proof and covariance nullspace characterization with a nonsymmetric counterexample. |
| 8 | SVD, pseudoinverse, conditioning, stable least squares | taught | SVD proof from AᵀA; numerical stability and rank-deficient solution ambiguity are treated explicitly. |
| 9 | Multivariable derivative and chain rule | taught | Fréchet chain-rule proof, matrix shape exercise and partials-without-differentiability counterexample. |
| 10 | Hessian, Taylor bounds, constrained extrema | taught | Taylor remainder proved via one-dimensional integral remainder; multiplier failure is constructed. |
| 11 | Double integrals and change of variables | taught | Local area interpretation, theorem with conditions and proof sketch, and explicit many-to-one failure case. |
| 12 | Completeness and convergence of real sequences | taught | Supremum proof plus Cauchy, subsequence and recurrence exercises with explicit quantifiers. |
| 13 | Continuity, compactness, uniform continuity | taught | Compact sequential proof and explicit noncompact-domain counterexample. |
| 14 | Pointwise/uniform convergence and continuity | taught | Uniform-limit continuity proof, sup-norm exercises, and derivative interchange failure boundary. |
| 15 | Riemann integration and interchange | taught | Uniform integral bound proved; counterexample distinguishes pointwise convergence and integral convergence. |
| 16 | Cumulative linear algebra/calculus/analysis readiness | taught | Theorem and 10 original A/B gate exercises; sessions schedule five A tasks with B substitutions. |
| 17 | Probability models, event algebra and counting | taught | Axioms/addition rule and five tasks spanning proof, counting, and nonuniform models. |
| 18 | Conditional probability, Bayes and independence | taught | Partition proof, base-rate example, and explicit distinction between independence and mutual exclusivity. |
| 19 | Discrete random variables and moments | taught | Expectation/variance identities proved; zero-covariance dependence counterexample and indicator transfer included. |
| 20 | Continuous/joint variables and density transforms | taught | Transformation derivation, triangular support, conditional density, and dependence counterexample. |
| 21 | Markov/Chebyshev concentration | taught | Both inequalities proved from nonnegative variables; loose-bound and sample-size examples included. |
| 22 | WLLN and CLT bridge | taught | WLLN proved via Chebyshev; CLT normalization, finite-sample approximation and dependence failure separated. |
| 23 | Finite Markov chains and invariant measures | taught | Invariant-distribution proof, detailed-balance criterion and periodic nonconvergence counterexample. |
| 24 | Sampling distributions, estimation, confidence intervals | taught | Sample-mean moments proved; MSE, prediction target and coverage interpretation exercised. |
| 25 | Likelihood, MLE, identifiability | taught | Gaussian mean MLE proof, boundary MLE, posterior distinction and nonidentifiable parameterization. |
| 26 | Sufficiency and Rao–Blackwell | taught | Factorization and variance decomposition proofs; completeness boundary is treated honestly. |
| 27 | Testing, likelihood ratios and error control | taught | Neyman–Pearson proof, p-value distinction, family-wise error and predeclared-tail transfer. |
| 28 | Bootstrap/permutation and undergraduate-to-graduate bridge | taught | Finite-group validity proof sketch, exact small permutation orbit and serial-dependence failure derivation. |
| 29 | Probability spaces, events, measurable maps, pushforward laws | taught | Definition and fully proved integration identity; five exercises include transformed and joint laws. |
| 29 | Carathéodory extension theorem proof | deferred | Probability measures are the starting structure; extension proof is named as beyond this sequence. |
| 30 | Simple integral, nonnegative and signed expectation | taught | Definition, monotonicity proof, dyadic approximation, and signed divergence exercise. |
| 30 | Outer-measure construction of Lebesgue measure | deferred | Probability measures are starting objects; Carathéodory construction proof is beyond this selected core. |
| 31 | MCT and Fatou for nonnegative functions | taught | Fatou fully derived from MCT; exercises prove it and test strictness. |
| 31 | Reverse Fatou with integrable domination | taught | Derived by applying Fatou to Y−X_n with finite subtraction checked. |
| 32 | DCT and expectation-limit interchange | taught | DCT hypotheses, Fatou proof sketch, strict failure and parameterized transfer. |
| 32 | Tonelli/Fubini and infinite-sum interchange | taught | Rectangle section calculation, absolute-integrability boundary, and nonnegative-series proof. |
| 33 | Lp, Hölder, Jensen, Cauchy–Schwarz and moment interpolation | taught | Hölder proved from Young; five exercises include strict Jensen and an L¹∖L² counterexample. |
| 33 | Completeness and general duality of Lp | deferred | Norms are defined; Banach-space completeness and general dual-space theorem are not claimed. |
| 34 | Independence, product factorization, and both Borel–Cantelli lemmas | taught | Both lemmas fully proved; exercise transfers summable errors to a.s. convergence and includes a dependence counterexample. |
| 34 | Zero-one laws and large deviations | deferred | These are outside the selected 16-week graduate-probability core. |
| 35 | Conditional expectation via RN and a.s. uniqueness | taught | Existence is explicitly assumed and sourced; uniqueness is fully proved; finite-partition and null-version tasks are scheduled. |
| 35 | Proof of the RN theorem | deferred | RN is an advanced named black box; no proof claim. |
| 36 | Tower, bounded pull-out, conditional identities and L² projection | taught | Tower and L² orthogonality fully proved; finite partition risk is derived; bounded pull-out is proved by simple approximation. |
| 36 | General Banach-space conditional expectation | deferred | Only the standard L¹ definition and L² projection are covered. |
| 37 | a.s., probability, Lp and distributional convergence | taught | Definitions, proved a.s.⇒probability and Lp⇒probability, plus rare-event converse failure. |
| 37 | Portmanteau theorem and weak topology | deferred | Distributional convergence is defined; general Portmanteau proof is reserved for an advanced probability sequence. |
| 38 | Uniform integrability, expectation tails and Vitali L¹ convergence | taught | UI definition, spike counterexample, bounded-core lemma and truncation proof sketch with exact source. |
| 38 | Dunford–Pettis theorem and weak compactness | deferred | Beyond this probability/statistics sequence; UI is used only in the scalar Vitali theorem. |
| 39 | Weak LLN under finite variance | taught | Chebyshev proof required as a scheduled derivation and applied to Bernoulli samples. |
| 39 | Strong LLN iid finite first moment | introduced | Exact theorem and moment condition stated, sourced as assumed; full truncation proof is beyond this sequence. |
| 40 | Weak convergence, characteristic functions, CMT and Slutsky | taught | Definitions, Slutsky proof sketch with exact black-box boundary, five application/proof/counterexample tasks. |
| 40 | Portmanteau theorem proof and tightness theory | deferred | Used only as a named characterization in the sourced Slutsky argument. |
| 41 | iid finite-variance CLT and characteristic-function derivation | taught | Second-order expansion and nth-power limit are derived; Lévy continuity is explicitly assumed. |
| 41 | Berry–Esseen rates, Lindeberg triangular-array CLT, stable-law limits | deferred | Finite-variance iid CLT is the selected core; rates, arrays and heavy-tail limit laws are extensions. |
| 42 | Finite Markov chains, invariant laws and mixing | taught | Stationarity calculation, fully proved minorization contraction/uniqueness, geometric bound, periodic counterexample. |
| 42 | General-state recurrence, continuous-time chains and ergodic theory | deferred | Beyond the finite-state selected process core. |
| 43 | Filtrations, adapted martingales, increment orthogonality, bounded optional stopping | taught | Bounded stopping fully proved via stopped increments; conditional orthogonality proved; unbounded first-hit failure is explicit. |
| 43 | Doob convergence/maximal inequalities and general optional-stopping criteria | deferred | Only finite-horizon optional stopping is proved; broader convergence and UI stopping criteria require an extension sequence. |
| 44 | Probability-space, integration, convergence, LLN/CLT and conditional-expectation synthesis | taught | Five scheduled form-A tasks and five distinct form-B replacements span measure, CE/L², limit laws, Markov chains, and martingale stopping. |
| 44 | Full graduate probability prelim syllabus | introduced | Gate tests selected first-course graduate core only; Levy processes, Brownian stochastic calculus and general martingale convergence remain extensions. |
| 45 | Likelihood, identifiability, finite factorization criterion and exponential-family statistics | taught | Factorization criterion proved both ways; Bernoulli/Poisson examples and an insufficient-statistic counterexample. |
| 45 | Minimal sufficiency in general dominated models | introduced | Sufficiency is taught; minimal-sufficiency likelihood-ratio criterion is reference material, not claimed as proved. |
| 46 | Rao–Blackwell variance improvement and completeness uniqueness | taught | Variance decomposition proved; completeness uniqueness proved; UMVU theorem derived conditional on a named completeness result. |
| 46 | General characterization of complete sufficient statistics | introduced | Selected binomial family used; broad exponential-family completeness classification is not claimed. |
| 47 | Neyman–Pearson simple hypotheses and likelihood-ratio ordering | taught | Optimality inequality proved; randomization and composite-alternative failure addressed in five scheduled tasks. |
| 47 | Uniformly most powerful tests for broad composite families | introduced | Scope and additional monotone-likelihood-ratio condition named; general theory deferred. |
| 48 | Score, Fisher information, score identity and scalar Cramér–Rao | taught | Score identities and Cauchy–Schwarz proof; Bernoulli equality and Uniform support failure. |
| 48 | Information inequalities for irregular/nonparametric models | deferred | Classical regular scalar bound only; irregular and semiparametric bounds are extensions. |
| 49 | M-estimator consistency and uniform laws | taught | Argmax theorem proved; finite-net route and pointwise-only counterexample supplied. |
| 49 | General noncompact argmax theorem | introduced | Compactness supplies separation; coercivity alternatives are extensions. |
| 50 | Smooth M-estimator asymptotic normality and sandwich covariance | taught | First-order expansion derived from stated score CLT and uniform derivative LLN; correct and misspecified cases compared. |
| 50 | Semiparametric efficiency and dependent-score limits | deferred | Finite-dimensional iid estimating equations only; broader efficiency and dependence theory are extensions. |
| 51 | First-order scalar delta method and second-order degenerate case | taught | Taylor remainder proof, log/proportion transfers, and zero-derivative chi-square scaling. |
| 51 | Nondifferentiable directional delta method in general | introduced | Absolute-value limit follows directly by continuous mapping; general directional theory is not claimed. |
| 52 | Nonparametric bootstrap for the iid mean | taught | Conditional variance and CLT target derived; conditional triangular-array CLT explicitly cited as theorem. |
| 52 | Exact permutation tests and randomization inference | taught | Finite-orbit rank proof, worked assignment enumeration, sharp-null requirement and unequal-variance caveat. |
| 52 | Dependent bootstrap methods | introduced | An exact perfect-dependence counterexample is shown; block-bootstrap theory is deferred. |
| 53 | Kernel density estimation bias, variance and bandwidth rate | taught | Convolution/Taylor bias and variance derivation, MSE optimization, boundary and dimension examples. |
| 53 | Uniform density confidence bands and adaptive bandwidth selection | deferred | Pointwise leading-order behavior only; empirical-process bands and adaptation require broader theory. |
| 54 | Two-point minimax lower bound under squared error | taught | Testing reduction proved; Bernoulli KL/Pinsker example provides an explicit 1/n lower bound. |
| 54 | Fano packing bounds and broad nonparametric minimax rates | introduced | The two-point core is taught; multiway packing/Fano and full function-class rate proofs are extensions. |
| 55 | Gaussian covariance Frobenius risk and deterministic-target shrinkage | taught | Exact known-mean risk proved from Gaussian fourth moments; bias-variance formula and oracle weight derived. |
| 55 | Spectral-norm concentration and adaptive shrinkage optimality | deferred | No matrix concentration or universal practical weight theorem is claimed. |
| 56 | Lasso basic inequality, cone, restricted-eigenvalue prediction and l1 rates | taught | Full deterministic proof with constants, orthogonal-design computation and collinearity failure. |
| 56 | Debiased lasso, high-dimensional CLT and post-selection inference | deferred | Beyond the selected sparse-estimation result; no valid post-selection interval is claimed. |
| 57 | Beta–Bernoulli posterior, posterior mean and predictive distribution | taught | Conjugate update proved, numerical posterior and predictive tasks, pseudo-count interpretation. |
| 57 | General Bayesian consistency and Bernstein–von Mises theorem | deferred | A conjugate finite-dimensional model is taught; general posterior asymptotics are not claimed. |
| 58 | Bonferroni and Holm strong FWER control | taught | Both are proved by union bound with super-uniform null p-values; Holm numeric stepdown is worked. |
| 58 | BH FDR under independent tests | taught | Procedure and finite calculation taught; independence theorem has an explicit sourced leave-one-out proof sketch. |
| 58 | FDR control under arbitrary dependence | deferred | No Benjamini–Yekutieli dependence correction proof is claimed. |
| 59 | Studentized Wald asymptotic calibration | taught | Slutsky proof, numerical sandwich interval and regularity boundary. |
| 59 | Post-selection uncertainty and Bayes/frequentist interpretation | introduced | Explicit two-estimate undercoverage calculation and credible-versus-coverage definitions; general selective inference remains extension. |
| 60 | Inference gate coverage across regular, nonparametric and high-dimensional methods | taught | Two distinct forms each contain five complete tasks; every task has full solution, two-point rubric and one critical criterion. |
| 60 | Assessment integrity and remediation | taught | Forms have separate exercise IDs, explicit blueprint, 16/20 threshold, all-critical requirement and prerequisite-linked retest path. |

## Sources, versions and access

- **f-book-proof: [Book of Proof, Third Edition](https://richardhammack.github.io/BookOfProof/Main.pdf)** — 3rd edition, author-hosted PDF; free official; checked 2026-10-10. Chapters 1–5: logic, sets, direct/contrapositive/induction proofs, relations and functions; use the free PDF and the chapter links.
- **f-1801sc: [MIT 18.01SC Single Variable Calculus](https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/18-01sc-single-variable-calculus-fall-2010/)** — Fall 2010; free official; checked 2026-10-10. Units on limits, differentiation, integration, Taylor series; problem sets 1–5 and exam materials. Undergraduate reference.
- **f-1802sc: [MIT 18.02SC Multivariable Calculus](https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/18-02sc-multivariable-calculus-fall-2010/)** — Fall 2010; free official; checked 2026-10-10. Syllabus and units on partial derivatives, chain rule, Taylor approximation, constrained extrema, double integrals and change of variables.
- **f-1806sc: [MIT 18.06SC Linear Algebra](https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/18-06sc-linear-algebra-fall-2011/pages/resource-index/)** — Fall 2011; free official; checked 2026-10-10. Unit I four subspaces/rank; Unit II orthogonality, projections and least squares; Unit III eigenvalues, positive definite matrices; Unit IV SVD. Includes problem sets and exam solutions.
- **f-18100a: [MIT 18.100A Real Analysis](https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/18-100a-real-analysis-fall-2020/pages/lecture-notes-and-readings/)** — Fall 2020; free official; checked 2026-10-10. Lecture notes on real-number completeness, sequences, continuity, compactness, differentiation, Riemann integration, sequences of functions and interchanging limits; assignments 1–12 and final assignment.
- **f-6041sc: [MIT 6.041SC Probabilistic Systems Analysis and Applied Probability](https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/6-041sc-probabilistic-systems-analysis-and-applied-probability-fall-2013/pages/resource-index/)** — Fall 2013; free official; checked 2026-10-10. Units I–IV: axioms/conditioning, random variables, random processes, limit theorems and basic inference; lectures, problem sets 1–11, exams and solutions.
- **f-1805: [MIT 18.05 Introduction to Probability and Statistics](https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/18-05-introduction-to-probability-and-statistics-spring-2022/)** — Spring 2022; free official; checked 2026-10-10. Classes 1–27 and Units 1–4: probability, distributions, sampling, maximum likelihood, confidence intervals, tests, bootstrap and regression; problem sets and exams with solutions.
- **p-mit6436: [MIT 6.436J Fundamentals of Probability — lecture notes](https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/6-436j-fundamentals-of-probability-fall-2018/pages/lecture-notes/)** — Fall 2018; official MIT OpenCourseWare; free official; checked 2026-10-10. Lectures 1–9 abstract probability/integration; 16–19 convergence and limit laws; 20–26 processes, Markov chains and martingales.
- **p-mit18175: [MIT 18.175 Theory of Probability — lecture slides and assignments](https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/18-175-theory-of-probability-spring-2014/pages/lecture-slides/)** — Spring 2014; official MIT OpenCourseWare graduate course; free official; checked 2026-10-10. Lectures 1–10 spaces, integration, independence and Borel–Cantelli; 12–16 weak convergence and CLT; 25–35 martingales and Markov chains.
- **p-mit18655: [MIT 18.655 Mathematical Statistics — lecture notes](https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/18-655-mathematical-statistics-spring-2016/pages/lecture-notes/)** — Spring 2016; official MIT OpenCourseWare graduate course; free official; checked 2026-10-10. Lectures 2, 6–18 statistical models, sufficiency, estimation, minimax, limit laws, consistency, delta method, asymptotics and tests; 19–26 Gaussian/GLM examples.
- **p-cmu36705: [CMU 36-705 Intermediate Statistics — lecture notes](https://stat.cmu.edu/~siva/teaching/705/)** — Current public lecture-note sequence; accessed 2026-10-09; free official; checked 2026-10-10. Lectures 5–7 convergence/CLT; 11–19 sufficiency/estimation/MLE; 20–27 tests/bootstrap; 29–36 nonparametric, high-dimensional, Bayesian and Fano topics.
- **o-boyd: [Convex Optimization / Convex Optimization](https://web.stanford.edu/~boyd/cvxbook/bv_cvxbook.pdf)** — Boyd & Vandenberghe, 2004; author-hosted free full text and slides updated 2023; free official; checked 2026-10-10. Ch. 2.1–2.3 (convex sets/functions and first-order conditions); Ch. 3.1–3.4 (convex functions); Ch. 4.1–4.2 (problem form); Ch. 5.1–5.6 (duality, optimality, sensitivity); Ch. 9.1–9.5, 9.7 (unconstrained descent, gradient, steepest descent, Newton, implementation); Ch. 10.1–10.4 (equality-constrained minimization, constrained Newton, infeasible-start Newton, implementation); Appendix A.5 (linear algebra); Appendix C.2 (solving factored matrices), C.3 (LU/Cholesky/LDLT), C.5 (underdetermined systems).
- **o-prox: [Proximal Algorithms / Proximal Algorithms](https://web.stanford.edu/~boyd/papers/pdf/prox_algs.pdf)** — Parikh & Boyd, Foundations and Trends in Optimization 1(3), 2014; author-hosted final article; free official; checked 2026-10-10. §2 proximal-operator properties; §3 proximal interpretations; §4.1 proximal minimization; §4.2 proximal-gradient methods; §6 evaluating proximal operators (not convergence); examples for ℓ1 regularization and projection.
- **o-mit-ts: [14.384 Time Series Analysis lecture notes / Ghi chú bài giảng 14.384 Time Series Analysis](https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/14-384-time-series-analysis-fall-2013/resources/lecture-notes/)** — MIT OpenCourseWare, Fall 2013; free official; checked 2026-10-10. Lecture Notes 1 (stationarity, lag operator, ARMA, covariance); 2–3 (limit theorems, OLS, HAC); 4–5 (spectrum); 9 (bootstrap); 16 (empirical processes); 17–19 (unit roots, nonstationarity, breaks/cointegration); 21–22 (filtering, state space, Kalman).
- **o-cmu-mixing: [Time Series and Stochastic Processes lecture notes / Ghi chú bài giảng Time Series and Stochastic Processes](https://www.stat.cmu.edu/~cshalizi/754/notes/all.pdf)** — CMU 36-754 notes, chapter 27; accessed 2026-10-09; free official; checked 2026-10-10. Ch. 27, Theorem 396: α-mixing CLT; theorem proof is cited as technical and is intentionally assumed in this curriculum.
- **o-neweywest: [A Simple, Positive Semi-Definite, Heteroskedasticity and Autocorrelation Consistent Covariance Matrix / A Simple, Positive Semi-Definite, Heteroskedasticity and Autocorrelation Consistent Covariance Matrix](https://www.nber.org/system/files/working_papers/t0055/t0055.pdf)** — Newey & West, NBER Technical Working Paper 0055 (1986); published Econometrica 55(3), 1987; free official; checked 2026-10-10. Theorem 1 (finite-sample positive semidefiniteness). The general consistency result is not claimed as the lesson theorem; bounded m-dependent consistency is proved under explicit hypotheses in this lesson. NBER author working-paper copy is open access.
- **o-kunsch: [Simulation for Inference I — The Bootstrap / Mô phỏng cho suy luận I — Bootstrap](https://stat.cmu.edu/~cshalizi/dst/18/lectures/18/lecture-18.html)** — Cosma Shalizi, CMU 36-467/667, 6 November 2018; free companion lecture citing Künsch (1989); free official; checked 2026-10-10. The Resampling Bootstrap; Block bootstrap for time series. The original Künsch theorem remains cited as assumed; this lecture is not the original paper.
- **o-scoring: [Strictly Proper Scoring Rules, Prediction, and Estimation / Strictly Proper Scoring Rules, Prediction, and Estimation](https://sites.stat.washington.edu/people/raftery/Research/PDF/Gneiting2007jasa.pdf)** — Gneiting & Raftery, JASA 102(477), 2007, pp. 359–378; author-hosted PDF; free official; checked 2026-10-10. §2 propriety and logarithmic score; §3 continuous ranked probability score; exact log-score identity derived in this lesson.
- **o-sarkka: [Bayesian Filtering and Smoothing / Bayesian Filtering and Smoothing](https://users.aalto.fi/~ssarkka/pub/bfs_book_2023_online.pdf)** — Särkkä & Svensson, second-edition online manuscript, 2023; free official; checked 2026-10-10. Second edition, Ch. 5 (state-space models); §6.3 (Kalman filter); §§12.1–12.3 (smoothing equations and RTS); §16.3 (practical parameter estimation). Chapter/section numbers follow the 2023 second-edition contents, not the 2013 first edition. Author-hosted personal-use PDF; lesson text and exercises here are original.
- **o-cmu705: [36-705 Intermediate Statistics lecture notes / Ghi chú 36-705 Intermediate Statistics](https://stat.cmu.edu/~siva/teaching/705/)** — CMU, S. Balakrishnan course notes; accessed 2026-10-09; free official; checked 2026-10-10. Lectures 2–10: concentration, convergence, uniform laws, applications, Rademacher complexity; Lectures 26–27: bootstrap; Lectures 30–32: high-dimensional and nonparametric regression.
- **o-hazan: [Introduction to Online Convex Optimization / Introduction to Online Convex Optimization](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1909.05207)** — Elad Hazan, second edition, arXiv:1909.05207v3, 2023; free official; checked 2026-10-10. Ch. 2.3 (constrained gradient descent); §3.1 (online gradient descent); §3.3.1 (strongly convex losses); §10.1 (dynamic regret). The path-length OGD bound used here is derived in this lesson.
- **o-hansen-hac: [Consistent Covariance Matrix Estimation for Dependent Heterogeneous Processes / Consistent Covariance Matrix Estimation for Dependent Heterogeneous Processes](https://users.ssc.wisc.edu/~behansen/papers/ecnmt_92.pdf)** — Bruce E. Hansen, Econometrica 60(4), 1992, pp. 967–972; author-hosted PDF; free official; checked 2026-10-10. Entire six-page article (pp. 967–972); primary reference for general kernel covariance consistency. This lesson proves only its explicitly stated bounded m-dependent special case and does not claim Hansen’s general theorem.
- **o-bandit: [Bandit Algorithms / Bandit Algorithms](https://tor-lattimore.com/downloads/book/book.pdf)** — Lattimore & Szepesvári, 2020 free online edition, author-hosted; free official; checked 2026-10-10. Free online edition (same content as print, corrected minor typos): Ch. 1.1 (protocol); §§2.3–2.6 and §5.3 (conditional probability/expectation and concentration); §§4.1–4.5 (stochastic model and pseudo-regret); §7.1 (UCB optimism); §31.2 (stochastic non-stationary bandits); §§34.3–34.6 (conjugate priors, Bayesian bandits/regret); §36.1 (finite-armed Thompson sampling).
- **a-filter: [Särkkä — Bayesian Filtering and Smoothing](https://users.aalto.fi/~ssarkka/pub/cup_book_online_20131111.pdf)** — 2013 author-hosted online edition; free official; checked 2026-10-10. §4.3 Kalman filter; §8.2 Rauch–Tung–Striebel smoother; Appendix A.1
- **a-prob: [MIT 6.436J — Fundamentals of Probability](https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/6-436j-fundamentals-of-probability-fall-2018/pages/lecture-notes/)** — Fall 2018; free official; checked 2026-10-10. Lectures 7–9 integration, 16–19 convergence/UI, 25–26 martingales; public exams as scope benchmark
- **a-stat: [CMU 36-705 — Lecture 19](https://stat.cmu.edu/~siva/teaching/705/lec19.pdf)** — Fall 2019; free official; checked 2026-10-10. §19.2 support failure; §19.3 asymptotic normality; §19.4 influence functions
- **a-convex: [Boyd & Vandenberghe — Convex Optimization](https://web.stanford.edu/~boyd/cvxbook/bv_cvxbook.pdf)** — 2004 book; official lecture index updated 2023; free official; checked 2026-10-10. §5.1–5.5 duality/KKT; §9.3 gradient descent; §10.1 equality constraints
- **d-matrix: [The Matrix Cookbook](https://www.math.uwaterloo.ca/~hwolkowi/matrixcookbook.pdf)** — Petersen & Pedersen, 15 November 2012; free official; checked 2026-10-10. Gaussian distributions, block matrices, matrix identities and determinants; background for the independently derived daily examples.
- **d-sure: [The James-Stein Estimator / Ước lượng James–Stein](https://stat210a.berkeley.edu/fall-2024/reader/jamesstein.html)** — Berkeley Stat 210A, Fall 2024; free official; checked 2026-10-10. Stein’s unbiased risk estimate (SURE).
- **d-conformal: [Conformal Prediction / Dự đoán conformal](https://www.stat.berkeley.edu/~ryantibs/statlearn-s23/lectures/conformal.pdf)** — Ryan Tibshirani, Berkeley statistical learning, Spring 2023; free official; checked 2026-10-10. Split conformal prediction and calibration rank.
- **d-tweedie: [Tweedie’s Formula — Stanford EE367 / Công thức Tweedie — Stanford EE367](https://stanford.edu/class/ee367/slides/lecture12.pdf)** — Stanford EE367, lecture 12, university-hosted slides; free official; checked 2026-10-10. Tweedie’s formula for additive Gaussian corruption; companion derivation, not the original Efron paper.
- **d-evalues: [E-values: Calibration, combination, and applications](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1912.06116)** — Vovk & Wang, arXiv:1912.06116; free official; checked 2026-10-10. E-value definition and arithmetic averaging under arbitrary dependence.

Each main-route day has one primary focus and four contextual tasks: concept, derivation, application and error analysis. Three companion tasks scaffold the same mathematical object; they are not advertised as four independently authored unseen exam questions. Twenty new topics replace repeated main-route sessions. Original A/B records remain in the canonical archive and syllabus criteria, but are not selected by the daily schedule. Only the optional foundation repair route intentionally reconstructs prior work. Skills overlap and must not be added together.
